ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u3	I would warn you that qualified votes can be taken in the course of today's sitting by electronic means. Therefore, from this moment the period of 20 minutes from the notice provided for in Article 119, paragraph 1, of the Regulation (9:37 hours) begins. Pending the determinations of the Conference of Leaders, which is currently under way, I am suspending the sitting. (The sitting was suspended at 9.38 a.m. and resumed at 10.03.)
ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u4	Ladies and gentlemen, the Conference of Leaders approved the timetable until 5 December. At today's sitting, the Assembly will discuss the draft budget and stability laws in the draftsman's text, in the absence of a mandate from the rapporteurs. In accordance with the 1999 and 2006 precedents, they will be deemed to be retabled (by 10.30 a.m.) in addition to the amendments rejected by the Commission, even those not examined by the Commission for temporal reasons. Since the Government has announced its intention to put the question of confidence in the draft Stability Act, the Conference of Leaders has set out the following scanning of the timing for discussion of the two measures. Following the speech by President Azzollini, who will report on the state of play, the discussion of any incidental issues will take place at the joint general discussion. For these phases, a total of 4 hours were allocated to the Groups. Following the Government's reply, the amendments and articles of the draft budget will be voted on. The general discussion on confidence in the stability bill - for which it will be divided between groups 2 hours and 30 minutes - will presumably begin at 4.30 p.m., after the presentation of the maxi amendment by the Government and the assessment of its financial coverage by the Budget Commission. The final explanations of vote will take place from 7 p.m., while the Senators call it is scheduled for around 8.30 p.m. The session will then be suspended to allow the Government to present the Note of changes to the budget which, as soon as forwarded, will be immediately referred to the 5th Permanent Commission. The Assembly will then proceed to the vote of the Note of variations and the final vote of the draft budget law, with the presence of the quorum. On the day of tomorrow - which provides for a single sitting with suspension between 2 p.m. and 3 p.m. - the report of the Assembly of the elections and parliamentary immunity on the contested election in the Molise Region will be examined. Where agendas are presented in contradiction with the conclusions of the Junta, explanations of vote will be referred to all the instruments presented. It has been established that the agendas will be put to the vote at 7 p.m. and therefore the Presidency is entitled to harmonise the timing of the debate. The timetable also includes, on Thursday 28 November, the examination of the Puglisi motion on the protection of children's rights. Next week will be discussed the motion Nencini on the relationship of affiliation between Italian parties and European parties and, where transmitted in good time by the Chamber of Deputies, the decree-law on the extension of international missions. At the opening of the afternoon sitting on Wednesday 4 December, the vote will take place for the election of four actual members and four alternate members of the Supervisory Committee on the Deposit and Loan Fund.

ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u8	Mr Calderoli (LN-Aut). Mr President, I do not want to slow down or disturb the work, but with regard to the speech that has just been read, there is a great deal of concern about the innovations that are being introduced. In fact, we have organized our work and the government's statements on the assumption that the question of trust is being raised. However, we have not yet reached that point. It is a well-established and always respected practice for the rapporteur's report to be carried out, namely the President of the Commission to report to us on the work of the rapporteur, and for the general discussion after which the amendments to the budget articles and the budget articles are voted. At that point, the Government can present - it is in its availability - a maxi amendment and on the same possibly request confidence. We have always waited for the time when the maxi amendment and the request for a vote of confidence were handed over. At that point, the House suspends its work and the President convenes the Conference of Presidents of Parliament's Groups to organise its work with regard to the discussion of the issue of trust. We are now approving a timing set on the basis of what the representative of the Government told us at the Conference of Leaders, but the request for trust must be formally presented in this House with the maxi amendment and the technical report, which must be entrusted to the 5th Commission; only after the discussion of the issue of trust can we begin. It seems to me that the timing of the eventuality changes a very sensible practice. In this organization of work I find that there is something which is also squealing in relation to the Rules of Procedure. Furthermore, I find it inappropriate that the Senate, which has shown that it has a well-functioning Rules of Procedure, should be debating a bill of stability in a few hours, in a House of Parliament, which will then have three-year value. I am very perplexed about the new practice that is being introduced, because in the future we could pay dearly for these forces and such sudden accelerations. [[Applause from the LN-Aut, FI-PdL XVII and LAG Groups.]]

Mr President, I have already spoken on another occasion on the timetable of proceedings, representing a requirement for you and the House: in the Committee on Justice we launched, essentially unanimously (there was only the abstention of the 5 Star Movement Group), an urgent measure which concerns, in line with certain protocols of the prosecutors of the Republic of the territory, the planning of the destruction of abusive artifacts. Between the approval of a decree-law and now the discussion of the bill of stability, that provision, which had already been included in the timetable of the work, has passed into the background. I realize that all the measures brought to the attention of this House are important, just as this measure is, but it has urgent connotations compared to others, because in following the chronological order, as some imagines to have to do, the destruction of so-called needy abusive artifacts is taking place these days. I therefore believe that in the responsibility of the Senate Chamber there is a decision on this measure, so that we can obtain the homogeneity in the territory of the criteria that must follow the planning on the killings. I therefore ask you, Mr President, to await the urgency of the measure, to consider whether it is appropriate to include in the timetable the discussion of this measure, which - I repeat - has been approved in the Commission with substantial unanimity, so that it can be dealt with as soon as possible with the urgency it deserves.

ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u36	Mr President, the Budget Committee has not finished its work, although it has examined the measure until 2.45 p.m. this morning. He could not conclude them because at 2.30 a.m. this morning the number of amendments which were still on the Commission's agenda was so high (in the order of several hundred) that it was in no way possible to conclude the work in the time allocated to us by the Conference of Leaders, namely 9 a.m. this morning. First of all, Mr President, I would like to thank all the Members of the Commission for their work. On the basis of their political positions, everyone has participated with passion and competence in the work of the Commission itself. For as long as the Commission has worked, until the last minute, the work has been carried out regularly on the basis of absolutely legitimate behaviour on the part of all, which has shown, in every part, the will to examine the text in depth. I would also like to thank the Government, which has been attentive and present and has made a serene and serious contribution to the work. I would also like to thank all the offices that have witnessed us with absolute dedication: this seems to me, Mr President, a fact that I must resign to them because the work done to the end is worthy of careful consideration. Of course, I should also like to thank the rapporteurs, whom I forgot: we have been in contact these days, but their work has even been impressive on the psychophysical level, and this must be done to them. The issues examined and the amendments adopted concern a number of important issues. First of all, there has been a very timely response - which can of course be assessed in accordance with the principles of each of the senators and senators - to the serious disaster that has recently struck Sardinia. That is why I felt obliged to thank all the Members of the Commission because, with speed and passion, they have allocated the first appropriations for the disaster in Sardinia. Equally important was an amendment approved by the Commission on the release of access to credit for small and medium-sized enterprises and households. This is a significant volume of stakeouts that, through the so-called leverage effect of credit, will be able to activate a valueable mass in some billion euros available to small and medium-sized enterprises and families. Another amendment approved, linked to a very important and important issue that the Commission as a whole, the Government and the rapporteurs with my modest contribution have addressed and resolved, was the one aimed at rebuilding the fund for non-self-sufficiency, with an implementation of a considerable amount of 75 million, over 25 earmarked for the restoration of the fund for home care for patients with very serious disabilities. Mr President, Senators and Senators, I should like to say to you that the amendment was, of course, shared with great passion and sincerity by the entire Budget Commission. Also important was the examination of the new text on the house, with a significant figure (in the order of hundreds of millions), placed for deductions and a possible increase of the deductibility of the IMU for IRES purposes. This last amendment was not approved, the amendment on the house was not even addressed: it had been tabled, but the progress of the work did not allow it to be approved, and yet this is a measure that has been for the Commission's attention in a significant way. Amendments on other issues of great importance, Mr President, had already been submitted to the Commission and should have been examined, but for reasons of time it has not been possible to do so, even if these are issues which the Government will well be able to take into account in the amendment on which it will put the question of trust which it will submit to Parliament. Mr President, as much attention has been paid to the issue of cover, so that the limited resources available are actually covered, so that the beneficiaries can be assured that the availability is actually covered by the Stability Act. One final point, Mr President: we have not even been able to conclude, always for reasons of time, the examination of the draft budget law and therefore also that is submitted to the Assembly in the text of the proposer, the Government, text which, after the joint debate, will have to be submitted to the Assembly for approval. This is the state of play, Mr President, that I will give you a reply for the continuation of the debate on the 2014 stability bill.

ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u54	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to remind all of us today that the law we are about to vote does not represent for the Italians only one of the main rules laid down by the Italian legal order aimed at regulating three years of the economic life of our country; it is not just another occasion of heated debates and evidence of strength among the political groups inside and outside the Chamber, in newspapers and TV talk shows aimed, more than anything else, at a sterile self-promotion in view of electoral campaigns. It is much more: the desire of a country to return to its head, to look with confidence towards the future, to leave behind one of the darkest periods of our Republic, and not only from the economic point of view. Parliament's responsibility today is not to extinguish this hope in the citizens of the old and new generations and to respond to the often desperate demands we receive on the net, in blogs, on the street, in demonstrations in the streets. The country, our country, in its social components, asks us for a law of stability that does not only look at public debt, but that promotes investment for businesses, requires more economic efforts to those who have more, who value human capital as the most important form of capital in the modern economy, through support for knowledge, research and education. We have a responsibility to adopt a text aimed at economic recovery, which challenges the negative forecasts of Italy's GDP growth, which was issued by the European Commission on 15 November. Today we should vote on a measure that reminds Europe, and in particular Germany, that investment stimulates growth, restarts consumption, consolidates public budgets. Ungrowth-free strict accounts endanger the Union's own standing, and Germany should become aware that continuing to impose its unreasonable austerity recipes will remain alone. In fact, it is worth remembering that in the last two years, although the tax burden has increased and the primary public expenditure has decreased in nominal terms, the ratio of public debt to GDP has increased by more than 12 points: 2013 revenues will be about 30 billion lower than the forecast that the Monti Government presented to Parliament at the time of its installation, before intervening with the manoeuvre of December 2011. The recession, in short, has almost completely frustrated that maneuver and gives us even a worse picture than what at the time seemed to be bad enough to induce, as we will recall, the newly installed government to intervene heavily. This shows that the problems of our public accounts are due to low growth lustres followed by two heavy recessions. If the economy does not grow, no matter how much effort is made on the expenditure and revenue side, the sustainability of public finance, measured synthetically by the debt-to-GDP ratio, does not improve. The most urgent need is now to support demand for goods in order to get us out of the recession, and to revitalize it we need to improve labour market conditions. Today, with my speech, I feel the need to tell citizens how the 5 Star Movement has lived all this with a sense of responsibility towards the country. The approach used in the analysis and lawmaking of the law of stability was that of the good father of the family, who, with deep attention and sensitivity, listens to the needs of his children in order to make the best use of available resources. We have opened up to dialogue with all social groups, we have assessed the demands of the various economic sectors, we have faced debates on conflicting interests, fighting even in our home in order to seek a synthesis. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Senator Fucksia, I invite you to conclude. FUCKSIA (M5S). Anyone who has been able to talk to us, write to us, communicate with us and find an interlocutor interested in accepting proposals and solutions. We have tried to manage with conscience the thin portfolio of a country that has been reducing for decades of bad administration and shameless denial of an economic crisis that gripped even the most lively and productive forces. The good father of the family, it is known, considers all his children equal, but when the economic possibilities are scarce and he is still called to make choices, the only criterion in order to be able to make them in a fair and just way is to satisfy in the first place the needs of the weakest children, those who need greater help. The 5 Star Movement has also been inspired by this principle in presenting and fighting for the adoption of amendments to a law of stability found cold and shortsighted in relation to certain social issues. First the weakest: this is our wind rose. This, therefore, is the rationale behind the legislative interventions of the 5 Star Movement, especially in some sensitive sectors such as health care. ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u70	Mr President, senators and senators, I am pleased to say that I am speaking after three weeks of intense work. I believe that I will make a rather different intervention than those of those who preceded me. First of all, I want to thank all the staff - the Commission, the Senate in general, the Groups - who have worked so hard these weeks, supporting us all in our demands. It was also a great work of secretariat: in fact, the presentation of 3,000 amendments - which mean that there was participation, that there was the possibility for everyone to express themselves, to make their contribution - involved a great deal of work. These are not only the initial 3,000 amendments, but also all subsequent sub-amendments, which were other thousands of contributions. This is a job that I still think is useful. It is true that perhaps we should have been more efficient and focused more on our work, but it was a useful job, because the debate was there. I am in a very different position from those expressed in previous speeches. Senator Mussolini said there was no respect. There have been so many respects: the Budget Commission has been conducted very rigorously and everyone has been given the opportunity to speak. The senators who were present in the Commission know that. The League is well aware that its representatives have been able to speak and express themselves whenever they consider it appropriate. I believe, however, that we should no longer waste so much time talking about rules and how to proceed, but we should start talking about content. I believe that this bill of stability can certainly be improved (we have seen it in the three weeks of intense work), but surely it has brought a first sign of change and reversal of trend: an opening of a new policy, linked more to the needs of the real economy and to development and less to rigour, even if rigour continues to be an essential element. We cannot allow ourselves to abandon the policy of rigour, which allows us to be within the rules of the European Union. On the other hand, I think there are the first good signs of policies in support of the economy: resources for development, starting with the increase in the additional budget of the Development and Cohesion Fund and many other interventions that have been made. The amendments examined in the Commission's work have proposed interesting changes in this respect, and many have also been accepted. I believe that the focus on development must be very high. Already previous interventions have focused on the enormous problem of youth unemployment. I do not dwell on all the other problems of the workers and non-employed in our country, but I believe that the problem of youth unemployment must really be addressed with great serenity: we risk losing a generation that will in part go abroad. Therefore, the rules already contained in the Stability Act, but above all the amendments tabled went in this direction. Another important chapter is investment, which is certainly a lever of our economy: public investment must be unlocked, as must local authorities. In this direction, efforts have also been made to reduce the constraints of the Stability Pact for local authorities in order to give a boost. In the law of stability in particular some major investments have been mentioned, and, again, the proposed amendments concerned important infrastructure for our country. Particular attention has also been paid to measures on the protection of the environment and the territory, and alas - because it is extremely topical - to the whole problem linked to the management of the situation in Sardinia. I do not want to dwell any further, because unfortunately the times are very short, but I still remember the social measures, which are fundamental to our party. They are the other aspect of the medal: on the one hand, a major support for employment and businesses; on the other hand, social measures that help those who do not succeed. We must never leave anyone behind: a civilised country is the one who is careful never to leave anyone behind; the smallest of our citizens must be supported. Finally, I would like to say a few words about the TRISE. This is an important chapter, which has been central: we have thought a lot about it; many proposals have arrived, and surely in the maxi amendment there will be new proposals. I believe that in the end a balanced solution has been found, which is why we will be able to support this law of stability, even more so after we have seen the government's maxi amendment (which I hope and trust takes into account all the comments made in the work of the Budget Committee), and I believe that my whole Group will do so with conviction. ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u80	Mr President-in-Office of the Senate, ladies and gentlemen, the ongoing phase of difficulties experienced by our country, among its many effects, has to place on the measure under discussion multiple expectations in order to identify the appropriate measures to restart production, reducing the high level of unemployment which has particularly affected the youngest sections of the population. Moreover, to resume to grow means to increase the level of social well-being, but above all it means to have the resources available to reduce, through a serious revival of income policy, the increasing and no longer tolerable income and social inequality present in our country. In my opinion, there are lights and shadows on the measure; the Group of the Party of European Socialists will support it and I will vote for it: certainly we cannot disagree with those who too easily criticized the deliberate content of the bill of stability. This text moves within the parameters expressed in terms of public finance balances already defined in the discussion of the DEF and revised by virtue of the economic performance with the Update Note. It should be remembered that these parameters stem from our membership of the European Union and the resulting financial constraints; however, well aware of the role played by the State Accounting Reform Act, introduced in 2009, by the Stability Act, one cannot fail to highlight the critical issues. It is useful to remember that with the law we are called to adapt, on the basis of a regulatory framework to existing legislation, the financial quantities to the public finance objectives already set with the DEF and the Update Note. If the measures which will attempt to initiate a new and modern public intervention for the revival of the Italian economy will be the subject of discussion in the examination of the connected sects announced by the Government, it is true that one cannot fail to point out that through the article under discussion rules could be introduced to stimulate the economic recovery, as well as to perform their natural function of quantitative regulation of the financial quantities and definition of measures of a general nature. With the rules introduced with regard to increasing the deduction on earnings from employees (the so-called tax wedge), corrective action has rightly been taken to concentrate these resources, but I believe that one could, even on the basis of a debate that has developed within the majority forces, better concentrate those resources, because intervention in support of demand or has a volume that is significant, with an impact on the economy, or otherwise risks having a really very low system effectiveness. In fact, from this point of view it must be borne in mind that another aspect must be considered: the problem of the propensity to save, which indicates even more the difficulty of making this intervention effective. In this respect, it is necessary to mention the data of the Observatory on household savings, according to which, in a difficult economic environment, households are rebuilding their savings and in the next three years the share of disposable income devoted to savings should therefore increase. The propensity to save in the course of 2013 is expected to increase by about one percentage point and - the Observatory concludes - the share of disposable income dedicated to saving will grow again in the next three years. According to these data, it would have been more useful then to concentrate resources and try to lower the level of taxation on our companies, intervening on the deductibility of labour costs and on the basis of IRAP. It is not possible to continue to have an IRES tax at 27%, when in Ireland, for example, the corporate tax is at 12.5%. In addition to this sector, on which we should have concentrated our resources better, we should also have concentrated our funds on competitiveness, because even large volumes of resources supporting demand do not mean intervention on competitiveness. With regard to export, 2013 is characterized by signs still positive, although much lower than those of 2012 and, therefore, worrying about the capacity of our industrial and manufacturing system, which still represents the economic heart and the engine of our development, on which we had to intervene more punctually It seems to me that some opportunities have been wasted. I hope that the government will focus more on this and correct some aspects of the maneuver. [[Applause from the PD Group]]

We are part of the European Union. When one company is not well there are usually two possibilities: one is the controlled administration and the other is a restructuring project. The restructuring plan consists of submitting to creditors the closure of an installation, or the purchase of a patent or an investment to obtain a boost. If those who submit the project are credible, it may be that creditors trust them, perhaps by funding them and allowing them to turn. Being subject to controlled administration means someone controls you and if you spend a euro you have to say where you get it, otherwise they block you. With regard to the European Union, years ago, France and Germany requested, on the basis of a project, that the 3% limit be exceeded in the debt-to-GDP ratio. It was granted to them. France has used this confidence badly and now finds itself in difficulty enough; Germany has used it well, has gone over, has returned and now things are going well: it has made reforms, among other things under the leadership of Schröder, a socialist, who has presented a programme of great restructuring, even with social sacrifices. We, on the other hand, are on the side of those who have taken over the administration. As soon as we cut a tax or schedule a grocery store, they ask us how we cover that grocery store. ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u100	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, no one would have imagined, on 16 November 2011, the date of the inauguration of the Monti Government, put in place by the President of the Republic with meticulous care and emphasis to save Italy, which after two years our The country would have been in the disastrous economic and financial condition in which it finds itself today. I believe that the debate on the law of stability, even though it is compressed, is the right opportunity to assess the results achieved. There are objective parameters that are able to make everything clear and unambiguous. In November 2011, the Italian public debt was 1,905 billion euros, as certified by the Bank of Italy on 15 January 2012 in its bulletin. Today the public debt certified on 13 November 2013 also of the Bank of Italy, referred to the month of September, is of 2,068 billion euros, after that in June it has touched 2,076 billion euros. Given that in these two years the public debt has increased to an average of 7-8 billion per month, adding to what matured 15-16 billion for the months of October and November, in the last two years the public debt has risen by about 180 billion euros: a good result after two years of tears and blood. GDP in 2011 grew by 0.4 percent compared to 2010, in 2012 by -2.3 percent compared to 2011 and in 2013 -1.9 percent compared to 2012: 65 billion GDP lost in two years. Unemployment was 9.6 percent in 2011; in 2013, in September, we are at 12.5 and at the end of the year it will reach 13 percent. The tax burden in 2011 was 42.5 percent, today we are 44.3 percent. Although primary government expenditure remained broadly unchanged in nominal terms, the ratio of government debt to GDP increased by 13 points and rose from 120% to 133.7 percent as expected at the end of the year. These are the figures of the economic disaster that has taken place over the last two years. It is clear that policies that are operated exclusively on the basis of wild taxation have led to a heavy recession. The written action to increase taxation indiscriminately has proved useless and counterproductive. In these two years the cuts in public spending have been fictitious and ludicrous, much less you have acted to eliminate waste, maliciousness and corruption, because we have to tell us: in the field of public spending they hide power, clientele and consent, to which it is difficult to give up these times. You've wasted two years. Compared to this increasingly dramatic and progressively worsening situation, the Government continues to remain inert. The 2014 law of stability that we are discussing could be an opportunity to give a turning point, to give a shock, as had been said, to the economy, while it is yet another missed opportunity. I do not know what you are going to do in Brussels with this law, which would be more correct to call a thousand marchettes. [[Applause from the LN-Aut Group.]] Small things can fit in a law, but let's put them in other specific laws, let's go to Brussels with our precise targets of debt containment and public finance. These days I have not heard a party, a union, a trade association, a newspaper, a citizen express a positive opinion on this bill. In the economic context, it is clear, there are only higher revenues: 7 billion in 2014, 16 billion in 2015, 19 billion in 2016, and it is all underestimated, because then the higher revenues will be more. On the other hand, there was a need for strong cuts in public expenditure to cover lower taxes and taxes on labour and businesses. Less taxes boost competitiveness and demand at the same time. It is necessary to improve the competitiveness of companies by lowering the cost of labour per unit of product compared to other European countries in order to intercept at least the demand that comes from abroad, because in Italy everything is still. Domestic demand had to be supported, with more money in paychecks: 1.5 billion are laughable. On the other hand, you have increased VAT, leading to a reduction in consumption and revenue: VAT revenues, despite the increase in the rate, have decreased, with a substantial loss of €30 billion in terms of lower overall revenue. The spending review is still on the pole. Before we paid Commissioner Enrico Bondi and now Commissioner Carlo Cottarelli, but how, where and when to cut there is no trace. Even the reform of the Provinces has not been successful, despite the fact that you have been talking about it for years. We asked you to raise the area to 12,000 euros for all low-income categories (employees, pensioners, self-employed), because you can't call to pay taxes who earns 650 euros per month. You said no. We asked you to eliminate the IMU on the first house, on the land, on the agricultural buildings and on those in which the productive activities are carried out: how does a company pay the IMU on the building where it works, if it does not work, since the production of thousands of farms is stopped? You have given us a new tax, the IUC, which will lead to a higher levy of 10 billion: you will see that under regime, compared to the 20 billion paid in 2013 net of the tax on the first house, we will reach 30 billion. It was necessary to support construction, which is a driving sector, around which there are about 37-38 other side activities: this sector is being massacred instead. Structural reforms were needed to improve the competitiveness of the system: no reform has been made. The government continues to live on the day, continues to float on the problems, continues - and this irritates - to play with communication, through press conferences, messages and proclamations, which do nothing but irritate the people, families and businesses that find themselves fighting every day a thousand and more insurmountable difficulties. At least have the decency to keep quiet for a while. Finally, I would like to ask you: how are we going to reduce public debt in the relevant proportions imposed by the fiscal compact since 2015? When are we gonna think about this next year? We're not really there. We had expressed reservations about this measure since its presentation. In the Commission, we had hoped to improve it and, even if it did not complete its procedure, the measure has become much worse, enriching itself with those brands at the limit of provocation. In this regard, allow me to refer, by way of example, to the proposal contained in one of the sub-amendments to the text of the law. Public bodies in Calabria are expected to be allowed to employ workers from 1 January 2014, even in supernumerary positions, with an indefinite contract: therefore, while we have to cut public spending, we continue to hire people who do not serve. It is still expected, always with reference to the public bodies in Calabria, the concession of a sum of 40,000 million euros in case of voluntary resignation submitted by December 31, 2013 for workers who have exceeded 60 years of age: but the workers are all equal, whether they live in Calabria, Sicily, Marche or Tuscany. Although we do not know the final text that will be put to the vote in this House, the judgment on this measure is highly negative and will hardly be able to have our support. I welcome some of the speeches contained in both the text of the stability bill and in the amendments tabled by the Government (which I am sure will also be included in the maxi amendment that the Commission is going to table) because they resolve some problems, which we have already raised on other occasions. I am referring, for example, to the restoration of the facilities for small-scale farming, in the case of professional farmers. I have tabled a number of amendments, together with my colleague Mr Berger, for the restoration of facilities for mountain farmers. I hope that the special situation will be taken into account, as the Committee on Agriculture wishes. In the last few days we have also spoken so much about hydrogeological disruption, and so I hope that the House of Deputies, where our Group will resume these amendments, will take into account the particular situation and the fact that these facilities expire on 31 December 2013. I also express satisfaction for the reduction of VAT on social cooperatives, for the regulations for road haulage, for the restoration in part of the dignity of the taxpayer's Guarantor and also for the restoration of the facilities for agricultural diesel. I want to express bitterness only because I could not discuss an amendment proposing the merger of concessions around the Brenner Corridor. Last night we also presented a specific agenda for the Commission's proceedings and I hope that the Government will nevertheless take account of this call for the first time for all the motorway companies of a territory, the institutions and also various political forces together to share a project of strategic importance, not only for our territories, but for the whole nation and for Europe. ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u110	Senator Mandelli is enlisted to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u111	Mr President, representative of the Government, our doubts about the stability bill have increased over time. Over the past few weeks, a balance has been sought between two opposing requirements. On the one hand, there was ours, which was firmly aiming at a development policy which, as is well known, requires a reduction in the tax burden. The majority of Italian economists, also belonging to the left side, now agree on this requirement: I quote for all Tito Boeri and his corrosive interventions on "la Repubblica" precisely in the matter of the law of stability. Added to this are the European Commission's calls for the six points that accompanied the closure of the infringement procedure against Italy, to end with the International Monetary Fund (IMF), the OECD and the World Bank, which recently reminded us that the tax burden has reached the highest value in Italy in the whole of Europe, exceeding also the French one, traditionally much heavier. On the other hand, there was the line supported by the left-wing component of the majority, which has never renounced the principle of "tax and spending," a principle that is constantly justified by a need for equity that we do not, of course, underestimate. However, talking about redistribution of income only makes sense if, in the meantime, wealth production grows. If, on the other hand, the overtaxation hinders any possibility of development, then solving the equation of greater social justice becomes not only impossible, but counterproductive: it is transformed into a sort of expropriation to the detriment of the small and middle bourgeoisie, which is composed of those from whom a greater commitment to income production is expected. It is a real short circuit that risks degenerating into a real war between poor and less poor, as Tito Boeri himself denounced in the article I mentioned earlier. The contrast between these two different positions has become particularly evident in the course of the debate on the measure, when any possibility of countering a drift that went in the sense I just said has failed. The examples of what has happened are obvious. I am thinking of the will of revenge that has been expressed against the recent judgment of the Constitutional Court in the matter of pensions: it has been repeated, with a hint of extra cleverness, a scheme that the Court had already judged outside the scope of our Constitution, creating a potential conflict that certainly does not calm down. I would add that, once again, a retroactive rule seeks to affect citizenship rights which should always be safeguarded, because this architrave is the foundation of the rule of law. I do not dwell on the reduction of the tax wedge because of its substantial irrelevance. The bill of stability dilutes in three years resources that the Prodi Government had concentrated in a single year without achieving any acceptable results. What has been given is a small tip that does not solve any fundamental problem and that has fueled the criticism of all the social partners, from the unions to the Confindustria, not only because of the irresosibility of the amount granted (the price of a cup of coffee), but because of the absence of any economic logic underlying it. At least he had put it in some way in relation to underlying productivity, but also this solution seemed too daring to those who, consciously or not, have now rested in the wake of the philosophy of the so-called happy degrowth. But the worst disaster was the taxation of real estate. The new rules are likely to increase the total levy from EUR 24 billion to EUR 30 billion, not to mention the additional resources available to the municipalities: another billion and a half. It will focus on buildings other than the first house: in theory a fair solution, in fact a particularly heavy property tax, while the rest of the financial wealth enjoys lower exemptions and rates. This is the true paradox of the Italian situation vis-à-vis international: in Europe we are now the country that has the highest taxation on real estate - see the recent report of Mediobanca - with the exclusion of France and England, where however the level of services rendered is immeasurably higher than the Italian ones. The situation is paradoxical due to the fact that the real estate sector plays a significant role in the production of GDP. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Senator Mandelli, I invite you to conclude. MANDELLI (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, I would ask that I be able to hand over the text of my speech so that it can be published in the annex to the Minutes of today's sitting. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. The Presidency shall authorise it accordingly. MANDELLI (FI-PdL XVII). However, allow me to make a general reflection on what happened this morning. I am wondering from this morning whether the citizens ask us to have the ability to concentrate the work of Parliament to give a good response to the needs of the country in the matter of economy or if they wait quickly for the decadence of Senator Berlusconi. I personally believe that today we should concentrate on giving answers to the country, which is outside this House and which gave us an effort to get it back on track. For these reasons, everything that has happened this morning does not agree with me at all. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII]]

ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u132	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I agree with the statements made by the previous senators when they spoke of a lack of respect for Italian citizens. What is, or rather, what should the law of stability be? It should be the main legislation provided for by the Italian law to regulate the economic life of the country (repeat, the economic life of the country). Let's get into it specifically. Italy case: more than 6 million people without work; 9,563,000 people living below the poverty line; 4 million citizens who can no longer even feed themselves; 1,224,000 pensioners receiving income below 500 euros, followed by cash-integrated, precarious and exodus. Between 2012 and 2014 the contraction in consumption of Italian households, due to the non-existent income support and the total absence of serious economic policies and worthy of an advanced action, will amount to about 60 billion euros. The consequences of all this will be a contraction in the purchasing power of families, a reduction in domestic demand and therefore a decline in economic activity: a disaster! Economic life and Italian stability are a heap of rubble. So, what does the government do? On the one hand, with the bill of stability affects the weakest citizen and companies not providing substantial interventions; on the other, it provides a disastrous and devastating plan, called Destinazione Italia (in fact, better to say, Devasta Italia or Svuta Italia), which definitively affects the economic recovery of our country and at the same time plans its sale. It is the case that the Italian Government tells all Italian citizens the truth, that the sales have begun: Italy is on sale, indeed in sale. Who gave the government permission to do all this? The goods are citizens! The 5 Star Movement for several months calls for immediate solutions for the Italian economy and disaster. We call for citizenship income, the abolition of IRAP and all the other solutions provided for in our amendments to the stability bill. [[Applause from the M5S Group]]
ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u133	He's enlisted to speak Senator Zanettin. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u134	Madam President, this bill of stability - alas, Deputy Minister Fassina - appears dramatically far from the deep reality of our country. In words, everyone says that they want to implement a development policy, which, however, is not achieved by spending laws and new taxes, as this Government continues to do, but by laying down the conditions for companies to prosper. We must all be aware that wealth is not created by bureaucrats and politicians; only businesses and self-employment create new wealth, which can then be redistributed to the weakest. There is therefore an abysmal gap between what we are discussing today and the needs of the Italian productive world, the people of VAT matches, mainly made up of small and medium-sized enterprises, who still (who knows how much) survive among a thousand sacrifices. What does this bill of stability provide for them? Alas, I would say nothing! I come from a region, Veneto, where we observe daily, with bitterness and discouragement, that many, too many, decide to expatriate in search of a normal working environment, where the tax does not claim a percentage higher than 30% of the income, where the bureaucracy is efficient and collaborative, where the merit and the talent are really valued. Businesses are leaving, capital is leaving, the best young graduates are leaving, even freelancers and self-employed people are leaving. I wonder how we can think of an economic recovery if politics only thinks of new taxes and does not cut spending, as is the case with this bill of stability. If this country has a real hope of returning to growth and of saving itself from the catastrophe that looms, this can only come, in my opinion, from a shock, indeed I would say better from a liberal and liberal electroshock. I will try to make some rather radical proposals, knowing well that they will not be shared in this House, as they are totally minority, but I do so in order to throw a seed into the debate. Let's start with privatisation. In recent days the President of the Council has spoken of the imminent sale of minority shares of ENI, Fincantieri and other public companies. The intention is commendable, but the result in financial terms seems poor and not able to reverse the negative trend of our economy. Rather, we immediately cut public debt significantly, selling the entire control package of the State's participants without hesitation. We accompany, instead, the sale of the public real estate with a legend according to which the former barracks or the former hospitals for sale, notwithstanding the local regulatory plans, will immediately enjoy a urban destination of complete satisfaction for the buyers. No more tax cuts such as the new VAT rates, excise duties, mini-patrimonials and Tobin tax or the new single municipal tax (IUC) that discourage the economic initiative. Rather, we think of a marginal rate facilitated for those who, enterprise or private citizen, in the years to come increase, rather than decrease, their taxable income. We have appreciated the commendable intentions expressed in recent days regarding spending review, but it must be made clear that 32 billion savings in the three-year period 2014-2016 are too few. I remember that the state budget exceeds 800 billion a year. The cost savings that Cottarelli intends to make therefore correspond to less than 2% of this budget. To Cottarelli, who seems to me in difficulty, I would suggest rather to call at his side, as advisors, many Venetian administrators, who constitute a ray of hope, for administrative competence and civil passion, in a context of crisis so distressing. I can think of two examples of my territory, which I would like to mention in this House: the former president of the Province of Vicenza and the mayor of Zugliano, a municipality of 6,000 souls. The first since 2006, the date of first settlement, to date has reduced staff by more than 22%. Now in the province of Vicenza staffing expenditure is 19 percent of current expenditure, when the national average is 33 percent. In turn, the municipality of Zugliano, I repeat of 6,700 souls, has one employee every 293 inhabitants; the average of the Venetian municipalities provides one employee every 140 inhabitants, the national average one employee every 101 inhabitants, the Sicilian municipalities instead have one employee every 47 inhabitants. ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u136	I call the representative of the Government.
ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u137	Madam President, I thank all the senators for their speeches. First of all, I would like to thank the President of the Budget Commission for the work of these weeks, the rapporteurs for the text presented by the Government, all the members of the Commission and, with them, the offices of the Senate and the offices of the Ministries, who have allowed a job that I believe has been passionate, thorough, useful, which has allowed to improve the text of the Government, although, unfortunately, we have not managed to conclude the analysis of the amendments and we have not managed to reap the results of a debate that could have been, even in the final part of our work, just as fruitful. The law of stability is part of a difficult, sometimes very difficult, even dramatic framework for the conditions of the country's real economy, for the loss of work, for the difficulties of hundreds of thousands of young people in finding work, for the difficulties of businesses, the smallest in particular, for the difficulties of many families. It is also difficult because the intervention of the government and the economic policy of the government are part of a framework of economic policy of the eurozone that needs deep, even radical, corrections. Certainly the bill of stability that we examine today does not solve all the problems of the country and does not solve the most dramatic problems of the country. Yet I believe that, at a factual analysis of its contents, it is difficult not to recognize the sign of the reversal of trend compared to what has been done, at least, in the last dozen years. I see a quantitatively insignificant figure, but very relevant in qualitative terms, namely that for the first time for at least a dozen years, the bill of stability has an anticyclical sign: it gives resources to the real economy compared to the previous picture. An increase in taxes has been complained about in several interventions. I would like to point out that, albeit in a limited way, and with the Government's awareness that much more robust interventions would have been necessary, the stability bill reduces the tax burden on households and businesses: on household incomes through an intervention on the IRPEF which, as you know, has been improved in the Commission's discussion with the concentration on the lowest incomes, and made more important on that income bracket; it reduces the tax burden on companies, not only through the direct cut of INAIL contributions for a billion to grow in the coming years, but through a substantial strengthening of the so-called ACE, i.e. a tax relief that allows the capitalization of enterprises extended also to family enterprises, to individual enterprises, at a time when access to credit is more difficult. It also reduces to a certain extent not enough, I repeat, the IRAP for those companies that stabilize employment relations with permanent and loose contracts - a request that the Municipalities have repeatedly made in recent years - the Internal Stability Pact for one billion, allowing the Municipalities that have the opportunity to restart immediately the yards, the small works, with an important impulse to demand, a decisive factor in supporting the recovery of our economy. Before, Senator Barozzino stressed the problems of work: all these measures that support growth are obviously the necessary condition to generate work. It is clear that the objective of this Government is not to confine itself to finding resources for the integration fund by way of derogation; the objective is work, and work is built with a macroeconomic framework that supports a recovery that can feed labour. However, in the awareness of the difficulty, after having committed 1.5 billion additional resources on the Integration Fund by way of derogation for 2013, the Stability Act feeds with additional resources the Integration Fund by way of derogation for next year. On this basic plant, as I have tried to remind you, the Commission's debate has been fruitful and useful, with the contribution - I stress - of all the political forces present. It was a discussion that allowed us to strengthen the two axes that support the establishment of the stability bill: on the one hand oxygen for recovery through input and investment support; on the other hand, support for household incomes and equity, because today equity is the main macroeconomic variable to feed recovery. We unanimously approved - if I remember correctly - an important, emerging intervention for Sardinia, which allows us to give an instant response with resources immediately available to the populations affected by the tragedy of last week. In the parliamentary debate we put together a package of interventions not only for reconstruction, but also for prevention, for all the realities of our country invested by natural disasters in recent years. In the course of the debate, it was recalled that some amendments have been introduced in the Commission's work, which I consider to be very important in support of development and therefore work: a national system of credit guarantees for small businesses, which today is one of the main bottlenecks that inhibits and makes recovery difficult. I repeat that this is a national guarantee system, and not some additional resource on some funds available. We have introduced for the first time, with regard to those who talk about the sale of public assets, the State guarantee for credit to small businesses, through the Cassa depositi e loani. Banks that credit small businesses can transfer these loans to the Cassa Depositi e Prestiti, which buys them with the State guarantee. This makes it possible to significantly overcome or reduce the credit risk that has, in recent years, been one of the elements that has blocked access to credit to small businesses or has raised their costs unsustainablely. This is an extraordinary lever which will make the difference in the coming months for access to credit for small and medium-sized enterprises. We have refinanced the Central Guarantee Fund with additional resources, paying particular attention to the realities of the Mezzogiorno. It is not true, therefore, that there is no focus on the Mezzogiorno in the stability bill. The Central Guarantee Fund has special attention also because it uses funds for cohesion and development towards the Mezzogiorno. Finally, trust consortia have been strengthened, which, as you know, have played an important role in recent years, often replacing a difficult bank credit. There has been a substantial re-patriation of the trusted consortia with the involvement of the chambers of commerce, within the framework of the national guarantee system. I repeat, the term system is not emphatic: it describes the plurality of instruments put in place. Within the national guarantee system an intervention was made for access to the credit of families, for mortgages, for the purchase of the house, in particular for young couples. The third important point - and I start quickly at the conclusion - is the intervention on the house. Last night we tabled a proposal, which unfortunately we have not been able to discuss in the Commission. I think we need to talk about the data here, too. In the original text of the bill there is a billion more resources to the municipalities, aimed at reducing the tax burden on the house. I remember that the law of stability eliminates TARES; therefore, from next year, families will no longer pay it, and it replaces this revenue with a billion more resources. In the original establishment of the stability bill the taxation introduced reduces taxation on the first dwelling to a quarter. On a substantially similar tax base on the first dwelling, the standard rate increases from 4% to 1%. In the Government's amendment presented yesterday evening we took a very significant step forward: 500 million have been introduced that municipalities can only use for deductions to families with more difficulties or under special conditions that they intend to support. The tax system remains federal, and it is singular to hear, sometimes, from those who have shared in recent years a federal system criticism that give too much space to the municipalities to intervene. On this point of the housing tax I would like to point out that the text presented in the draft stability law contains the tenant tax to a limited extent to what was before the TARES, as regards the indivisible service component. In the design of the redefinition of taxation on the house there is no increase in tenants. Finally, there are three other important points, the first of which is the 100 million more for non-self-sufficiency. That sounds marginal. On the other hand, I believe that social policy is an additional signal. It has been many years since the Non-Sufficiencies Fund reached 350 million, and this has taken place in a framework where our difficulties and public finance constraints have certainly not eased compared to those of previous years. We have made important progress with regard to precarious workers. We have blocked the increase in social contributions on VAT consignments entered in the separate management of the INPS. We have given a response to the precariousness of justice, to some socially useful workers in more difficult areas such as Naples, Palermo and Calabria, to young medical specialists. We would also have liked to have discussed other important points if the Commission's work had come to an end. In the amendments reworded by the rapporteurs, there is an important, albeit experimental, intervention to combat poverty: the introduction of a minimum income for inclusion in some large metropolitan areas, which sets out a path to combating poverty and job integration. Finally, we have failed to take action to improve the indexing of pensions. It is a commitment that the Government confirms and that we will try to carry out in the House. The government hopes that in the discussion here and in the debate in the House in the next few days the two axes can be strengthened, that of support for the real economy and that of equity. Only in this way, with this work and with the involvement of all political forces, each with its own views and priorities, can we give the country the contribution it needs at such a difficult stage. [[Applause from the PD and NCD groups]]

ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u229	Mr President, two problematic aspects have emerged from the debate in the Commission. This was the first request. The other profile moves instead from an observation that has been made and that, I note, has absolutely well-founded traits. In addition to the type of drafting of the technical report, which can also be modified, but which, in essence, does not affect the content (it would be much better to modify it, because it appears more like a preliminary work to the technical report itself), I personally note that there is even a reference to a rule, on which for a long time it has been debated during the debate, then it appears in the text submitted to the confidence, so there is almost an overabundance of burdens. The norm is no longer there and there is a fundamental problem: the final prospect of the onerous effects is right that it makes more clearly emerge in accounting terms the most and the least, therefore the destination of the resources and the sources of coverage. Objectively, as one senator pointed out in particular, the final prospectus, even if corrected, does not make clear to the senators the origin of that balance. A balance of 10 can in fact derive from the difference between a +20 and a ‐10, or from a +30 and a ‐20 or even from a +100 and a ‐90. This is necessary in order to understand what, for example, the value of +100 is and what the value of ‐90 from which the balance derives. To write only that the balance is equal to 10 is not the task of the technical report; the task of the technical report is to ensure that that balance is the result of a burden and a cover. These are the two aspects highlighted by the Budget Commission: both are of relevance to the Commission and have been correctly identified.

We would like to imagine a country with a different articulation of the transports on road, but the reality is that of a sector that, besides employing such a large number of operators, guarantees with its work and its existence the functioning of our system Country. This is why the Commission's work is to be welcomed, although the resources that the rules we are preparing to approve free are not sufficient to cover the negative effects of the crisis that is being dramatically felt in this sector more than elsewhere. ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u298	Mr President, Minister, ladies and gentlemen, we are certainly in a delicate and important phase of the country's life, but like all difficult seasons, this phase must be dealt with with with a sense of responsibility, putting in place not pessimisms and defeatisms, but positive tensions, unleashes of enthusiasm and dedication to the cause and, therefore, to the country. It is with this intent that the New Right Center faces this new season, in an alliance of broad understandings that can be truly such until it is the alliance of individual claims: this is the fundamental difference that will have to distinguish the new season of this Government. It starts from a law of stability where the elements and the ferments of innovation are all grasped, and I must say, thanking President Azzollini and my colleague Santini, who were all emphasized during the debate in the Commission, and many have been consolidated by the results of the debate in the Commission and transposed into the text of the Government. Let us think, for example, of the initiatives on guarantee and development funds: never in my memory in the financial manoeuvres - I have done so many, perhaps I am the one that has done most of them in this House - had so many resources been included in the development funds with the mechanism of the multiplier of guarantee funds and trusts; it is EUR 800 million that will be able to develop at least 16 billion credits and investments. Never in the season of the last governments had a measure been adopted, which is not of this financial, but certainly fundamental for the future of the country, like that of the 40 billion for the payment to the creditor companies of the public administration. Never before had we faced an emergency in the immediacy and respond within twenty-four hours, as happened for the emergency of Sardinia, recovering 100 million euros real and assigning them thus to that Region, to whose state of difficulty we have associated and we associate with great friendship and a sense of solidarity. We have made a strong intervention in the field of infrastructure and road haulage. You see, Senator Di Giorgi, you said that you were surprised that in one day a political force, or part of it, would pass from the majority to the opposition, but we who have some experience, even with regard to our colleagues in this same political part, have already felt for days that this would happen. We have warned him in the proposition of modificative proposals of a purely opposition type, of that demagogy that we are accustomed to seeing in all oppositions of any political color, that is those proposals that are unrealizable, and unacceptable, but certainly of suggestive impact on citizens. I am thinking of the lowering of two points of VAT and the lowering of the tax burden, that is, those things that make hold, but that, being unrealizable, are not by government force, but by pure force and typical of the opposition. Therefore, we had understood this and we had the confirmation when yesterday, in order to vote against the Government and the majority in the Commission, this part of political force has even voted against the maneuver in favor of the road haulage, that it has been one of the traditional sectors of electorate of reference of the centerright. It is now, of course, still a centre-right, because there is a part of the centre-right that has promoted its defence and, through the Letta-Alfano agreement with transporters and the sanction in the Commission (and therefore today in the law of stability), has confirmed its content. So, we understood that very well and we are convinced that this is the right path. Friends of Forza Italia, this is not the Forza Italia that I knew twenty years ago: the Forza Italia of enthusiasm, hope, liberal sense, tolerance, dialogue and love for citizens, even at the cost of giving up something. It is not this and today you perfectly interpreted, instead, the typical part of the livorous opposition and without any constructive contribution. But let us go on, instead, with the things that we have achieved and with those that we intend to achieve in this short part of the legislature, because we are convinced that this is a transitional legislature and that tomorrow, once the political climate has been calmed down, we will also be able to distinguish, as we want, between different ideologies and values, but always in good faith, according to the moral and political patrimony of each of us. We have started an important manoeuvre in the fiscal part, we have also called for the inclusion, precisely according to that concept of broad agreements that must not consist in individual claims, but in a meeting of ideas, of some strong points of the programmes of the center-right, such as that of fiscal tolerance, with the introduction of a new and further opportunity for the extinction of the tax debt of the Italians, which we know is in many cases a debt of necessity and not a debt of evasion or a lack of willingness to pay taxes. We have also launched, beyond what is said in the agencies and in the formidable construction of numerical multipliers, a new policy of taxation on the house, simpler, clearer, more direct, with precise exclusion from the taxation of the first house and with precise orientation, instead, not towards taxation but towards the contribution of the citizen to the services that the municipalities make. Not for nothing, apart from the figures that are built with the multiplier and that are outside of any reality, our maneuver on the house today is annoying, I would say indeed the strong irritation of the Municipalities that are the ones that have to use this taxation and that they thought and maybe think they can still continue to spend without roofs, like those that we instead obtained today that imposed themselves on the taxation of buildings and services. One of the results that I consider most important, in fact, even if you may not have read it among the lines of the new legislation, is the imposition by 2016, for some taxes that today are homeless, the observance of the standard costs and therefore the observance of the true relations between the service and the cost of an efficient management then to be shared among the citizens. We have also introduced 500 million deductions on taxation for services, in such a way that the weakest social groups may not pay anything or pay marginally; we have introduced for the first time in this law of stability, increasing it in the Commission on the impetus given initially by the Government, the deduction of taxation on industrial plants. Certainly it could not be done at 100% (this is a purely demagogic proposal or aimed at captivating the sympathy of one or the other category, which is made by those who are typically opposed and loses the sense of concreteness and politics, which is the art of possible, of dialogue and always find the best solutions, of course in good faith, that can be adopted). We have also introduced real elements of novelty in saving public expenditure. In fact, I agree with Senator Lanzillotta when she points her finger against waste and against many aspects that are still illiberal of our society and our economic structure, especially in the relationship between the public and the private, but this will be done and can be done gradually, without excess and with great determination and rigour. The companies involved must be reduced in size, the intermediate bodies must be abolished, the turnover in the public administration must be acquitted through the absorption of the staff of the bodies that must be closed and abolished, but above all it must be addressed in this country (sorry if every time I speak this button) the reorganization of the institutions in the territory. That is why I have proposed a number of speeches, which were then put on an agenda that I have tabled, which some might even consider provocative, but which are and must be discussed in the coming months, when we are dealing with major institutional reforms. We cannot continue to live with 20 Regions, 8,200 Municipalities and 110 Provinces in the process of dismantling, which are the real waste of the country. If we want to be reformers, we must really start a profound reform of the country's institutions: abolishing intermediate bodies, concentrating municipal realities on a scale and, for the economy and efficiency of services, the same regional realities in basins of users not less than 6-7 million inhabitants, thus leading our country to an authentic federalism at the territorial level and at the level of force that the same territories, not shattered in 100,000 institutions, can express. That is what we must start doing and we can do it, if we all together have the sense of responsibility not to send the country towards the crisis and not to let it proceed in the dark towards trials that we do not know where they can take us, defending it precisely through this attitude of tenacity. You see, Senator Crosio, I come from a city and a seafaring reality that much better than that from which you come can talk about ships, stormy seas and ways of leading the rudder and sails of the boats that face them: I assure you that the tenacity of the sailors is the one that always keeps the bar straight towards a goal, looking also at the possibility of helping someone who is in difficulty. We are convinced that we must work for the country and not create problems of crisis in the country at this moment, defending the legality, through our behaviour and also through our vote in this House on the law of stability. In the same way we intend to do tomorrow, in the face of a situation which we consider offensive to Italian democracy, such as that which President Berlusconi sees exposed to the risk of having to leave this Parliament, where we have been honored to sit with him and where we will be honored to continue to sit with him when he comes back. However, this does not mean abandoning the country to a crisis in the dark, of which we do not know what the developments could be, nor the dangers for Italy. We go forward in the interest of the country, always looking concretely, not only at the aspects we have mentioned, but also at the things that must be done. Certainly in the House this law of stability will have to be further improved. There are areas in which we have not been able to deal during these night hours in the Budget Committee, but which will certainly be taken into account in the House. Someone has delighted us and delights us, but de minimis is not curat praetor. We therefore go on our way, sure to operate in the interest of the country. [[Applause from the NCD and PD groups. Congratulations.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u307	Mr President, I too want to talk about this law of stability by trying to understand what law it is and can be, and that is what comes to mind: it is a law of stability at a time of passage, when the country and therefore its institutions, such as the Senate, Parliament, are called to interpret the meaning of this passage. We come from the swamp, from a very hard crisis that has lasted for six years now, which has deeply wounded the social fabric, the civil fabric, the families, the economy, the communities of this country. We know why it happened. We're not going back on it. We also know that with the same strength we must know how to get out of this swamp. We need to get our feet back on solid ground, start walking again. It seems to me that this law of stability must be interpreted in this sense: it cannot ignore that it is still struggling with the swamp and with the tight constraints of a path of rehabilitation that must continue, but trying to walk. I have listened to the debate very carefully and I wonder, and in total good faith and constructive spirit I ask our friends, how do they think that the more the catastrophe is exalted and the greater the seriousness of the situation is emphasized, the easier it is to imagine easy recipes, easy answers which, moreover, are well guarded then from providing. I believe that in total good faith and by comparing us we must start to say how to get out of this swamp. By reading in this key the law of stability, we find, and we try to find together if we are careful to look at them, signs. Signs, of course, not definitive and exhaustive but encouraging, that speak to the realities of families, businesses, work, institutions, the social discomfort that is inside the swamp and indicate, certainly still in a limited way, how many choices we had to make on these difficult nights to say that you could not have everything and that you had to choose where to allocate the few resources available to help those who needed more, to help really help the country to resume walking. Seen in this key, I believe that the bill of stability (and this I believe is indisputable even in today's eyes a little angry about those who criticize it unilaterally) bears signs. There is certainly a strong trace of relaunching growth and development, on which I do not dwell because others have already talked about it. There is a strong commitment to investment, guarantee, innovation, housing and infrastructure. All signs of development. There is a first sign of a reduction in taxation for businesses and jobs. It's not much. Of course, it's little, but let's not despise him. Who needs to despise him? A policy that must rebuild its credibility does not need to despise itself for low internal demagogy. We value this path towards reducing taxation so that we can continue it in the coming years before us. We are also talking to the local authorities and the institutions that are - it is true - prisoners of a Stability Pact that represents constraints but that with this bill of stability we try to start to loosen up. Isn't that a billion? Of course, it is little, but it still represents 25% of the Stability Pact. Next year we will do better, if we will be able to, but we do not despise what is there because there is and gives the opportunity to start to say that even the Municipalities resume to invest, resume to look to their territory in order to counter, for example, that dislike that many mourning, many tragedies has provoked and to which we have tried to respond with the measures in favour of Sardinia. We talked to the families, too. I say it with a certain force. For example, it has not come to light very much, but having provided for a very strong deduction in the new property taxation because lower-income households, with higher family loads do not pay these taxes, is a way to give them concrete help to keep up and to be able to look with less concern at the future. Finally, this cannot be denied, we have also tried to turn a look with different eyes to a world that is usually neglected: what presents serious social hardships, not self-sufficiency, disability. But there have also been small signs of Law 104, of biological damage, of compensation for those who died at work, for families, with the ability to speak also to poverty by beginning to experience active support for inclusion. Those are signs. Of course they are still weak signs, they are traces, they are not yet strong paths, but from here we start - in my opinion - to try to build in the political dialectic, even harsh but without escape routes. You see, the bar in front of us is very high and no one is allowed to do as that horse that in front of the high obstacle chooses to discard, to leave somewhere else making, in fact, recourse to easy promises or other. We must tackle this issue, tackle it vigorously with the certainty that it will still be a long and difficult path, but with the knowledge that it has been undertaken and that we must better complete - we hope - also through the debate that will take place in the House of Deputies. Everyone will be asked in the near future to continue to travel through it. I have in mind the spending review started so that finally needs and standard costs are elements that guide a country that wants to change its spending to qualify it and to reduce waste and inefficiencies, to recover resources to be allocated more forcefully to the reduction of taxes, to the creation of employment, to offer perspectives to young people and hopes for the future. It's a tall bar. We have signs and a long way to go. We can go through it if we believe in ourselves and in the possibility of really changing this country. If we do this and manage to do without the internal polemics and quarrels and division that we have seen in abundant doses during the examination of this bill of stability we will be able to change the country and the level of a policy that wants to grow and change.
