ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u311	FERRARA Mario (GAL). Mr President, since Senator Azzollini has extended the scope of the maxi amendment, I have to say that what we are doing has connotations of unconstitutionality. You, Mr President, have mentioned what the Offices have made available to you, namely the amendment of the Rules of Procedure of the Senate of 30 November 1988, which specifically states in footnote 3 that: The question of trust does not create a debate in its own right, but becomes the framework for the debate on the subject of vote on which it was placed. Previously it is stated that:... the question of trust is placed on the approval of an article or the single article of a bill of conversion of decree-law.................................................................................................... You mentioned these notes to point out that at this point nothing could be done by the Senate Presidency with regard to a document which was in the responsibility of the Government and that with the debate and vote required - as is used in the short parliamentary language - it overwhelms everything else. In this House, unlike the House of Deputies, no final vote is taken or agendas voted. I would like to stress to you, in order to give strength to my point and to my conviction, that what you have said is contrary, for example, to Rule 97 of the Rules of Procedure. In fact, if the maxi amendment contained proposals outside the scope of the debate and therefore contrary to the accounting law as, for example, legal or localistic, or if it contained wordings in improper terms, you would not declare it admissible. Therefore, there is no prejudice over the screening of eligibility. Having said that, Mr President, and since we are in any case called upon to vote, I imagined this speech as if President Letta were present, since Minister Franceschini had asked this morning for the organization of the work to be arranged so that President Letta could be present at the explanations of vote. I am convinced, like many political noticists, that everything comes from the lack of presence in the Government of the two leaders of that moment: one indicated by the people with its vote, President Bersani, the other, President Berlusconi, an undisputed leader, indisputable and inescapable leader of the center-right. If there is no peace, Minister Saccomanni, there is no possibility of exercising the difficult task of the unpopular government. The unpopular government is the one at Reagan or Thatcher, the one that makes difficult decisions to better build the future. The unpopular government is what it calls the best intelligences in the country to write about a new form of state and government, but it does not do so to add those pages to the thousand and a thousand pages already written. Those are the governments that promote development and do it by discontenting everyone a little, but they do it to favor and protect them all. Peacemaking, present Ministers and President Letta who surely listens to us, is the consequence of a difficult, continuous, prudent and courageous action; it is not and cannot be the management of events. The governments that manage and do not act are weak; perhaps therefore they may also be destined to last for a long time, but they are governments that do not inspire my trust, they are governments that do not inspire our trust. ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u313	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u314	ZELLER (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, the question of the government's confidence in the stability bill is, as the President-in-Office of the Council said, a political act for the verification of the majority. At the same time, it is a mandatory decision to ensure the time taken to approve the measure. We believe that our opinion on the Stability Act should be based not only on the assessment of the text under consideration, but also on a balanced and not a preliminary assessment of the government's action. Well, the President-in-Office of the Council and his Government have worked effectively, although political and parliamentary conditions are objectively difficult. There are few economic resources available. There are many European constraints and few growth prospects. The government was therefore obliged to carry out a delicate assessment of the compatibility of budgetary priorities to be safeguarded, but also of the necessary political support. Unfortunately, political conditioning was often inspired in the past by the achievement of propaganda and electoral aims and not by the common interest. This political part seems to have forgotten that in the last parliamentary elections no one has won, so it is out of place to stick to certain electoral flags, because we all know that Italy is not in the position today to make gifts, much less to everyone, rich or poor, as we have now decided to do on the first house. In this way we have only wasted valuable resources by charging the cost of the operation on businesses or other goods and services in a sort of inauspicious tour match where citizens still have to pay but in another way. This is a mockery and this is the best way to get civil society away from politics. Instead, the Letta Government has done well, starting the path of reducing the tax wedge with the objective of significantly reducing taxes on labour in the medium term, which is a strategic goal to become competitive in Europe and in the world. We also appreciate the stabilisation of measures for the energy recovery of buildings that will stimulate the construction sector today in severe crisis. In relation to the Special Statutes, the draft stability law under consideration presents many aspects that have highly risky constitutionality profiles, and that we, as the Group for Autonomies, have pointed out and strongly criticized. But during the examination by the Senate it was not possible to eliminate all the criticalities. Instead, for the first time in recent years and after a long period of stalemate, we managed to define a path with the Government in order to eliminate the frightening litigation before the Constitutional Court that has often seen the government succumb to the inoculate and often short-sighted political of the previous Governments, that have abandoned the path of the understanding and the agreement taking that of the unilateral imposition, of the diktat, seriously violating the prerogatives of the Special Regions. These government actions have almost all been sanctioned by the Supreme Court, so that this law of stability is also not based on very solid constitutional foundations. With President Letta and Minister Delrio we decided to change the register. The proposals we have indicated to the Government, as Special Autonomouss of Trentino-Alto Adige-Südtirol and Valle d'Aosta-Vallée d'Aoste, are consistent with the primary objective, shared by the President of the Council with the signing of the Bolzano Memorandum, to give full and real recognition and extension to self-government and, at the same time, to ensure our contribution to the consolidation of the state budget. A relationship of trust with the Government is indispensable for this path and also for the commitment to significantly reduce the dispute before the Constitutional Court today. Thanks to the constant commitment of Minister Delrio we are faced with important results achieved in recent months, with a very important reduction of the appeals presented by both the Government and the Regions. A further priority is therefore the resolution of this dispute also on the historical reserves and provisions in a short time, in the context of the implementation of the programmatic norm for the delegation of new state functions provided for in this law. Changes to the status of autonomy in respect of local finance and taxes, transfer or delegation of state functions and related financial burdens, understanding and definition of interventions capable of contributing to the extent corresponding to the achievement of public finance objectives: these are the issues we consider to be fundamental. In the past, the two provinces of Trento and Bolzano exercised competing powers in the field of local finance and local taxes, which led to continuous conflicts and was an endless source of mutual redress, also following repeated changes in the state's legislation on taxes on houses, waste and services. From 1 January 2014 we will have the primary competence in this matter. This means that we will be able to regulate this matter in full autonomy and also by way of derogation from state rules, ensuring not only efficient services and respect for the environment, but also stability, fairness and legal certainty for our citizens, which unfortunately have often been ignored at national level. In the law of stability, it was also possible to lay the legal basis for strengthening the agreements already in place with the Ministry of Justice for Peace Judges with the aim of extending this consolidated mechanism to the entire field of ordinary justice. It is now up to the Region of Trentino-Alto Adige/Südtirol to begin the agreements with the Ministry of Justice for a more equipped and fast justice. These are also factors that determine the competitiveness of a territory. A further positive novelty is the repeal of the inefficient management of the Fund for the Development of Border Municipalities to the special territories (known as the ODI Fund). On this point has been improved the agreement of Milan, with which this fund has been established, previewing that the 40 million euros annually allocated by each Province will finally be unlocked and from 1 January 2014 administered by the autonomous provinces, in agreement with Rome and with the Veneto and Lombardy Regions. The Valle d'Aosta and the Autonomous Provinces have already shown that they are able to assume their responsibilities and translate them into new competences, taking into perspective the burdens for all the state services in our Provinces. These are the basis for continuing in the direction of full and conscious autonomy. Instead of having to accept unilateral cuts from the state always looking for new and fresh resources, we are willing to take on the costs of state services and new competences, with the advantage, however, of being able to administer them in part at least independently. For these reasons it becomes essential that in January 2014 joint commissions are immediately established to issue the new implementing rules, with the delegation of the management of the Stelvio National Park, the functions for the Agencies of revenue, state property and customs, as well as for the management of administrative staff of justice. The Led Government and the majority have kept what they promised taking into account our demands by improving the text of the bill of stability. Now, after so many years of stalemate, we finally have a real prospect of implementing our common objectives, that is to increase autonomy for the good of all the linguistic groups present in our territories. Every day hundreds of our young graduates, a moment after completing their studies, take the first plane to escape abroad, thus dissipating an invaluable future resource for our country. Every month thousands of entrepreneurs close their companies, because they fail to collect the credits they have from their debtors, among which, very often, we also find the public administration. Every year, however, companies that do not close pay thousands of euros in taxes, such as IMU and IRAP. Resources that could remain in the pockets of the workers and that instead entrepreneurs are forced to pour into the account of a member, never seen and that does not participate in the activity of the company: the State. We hope, gentlemen, that the time still available to this soulless Executive will run out quickly. This stability law resumes a moderate investment expenditure; there is a moderate cut in spending that has taken place since 2015; there is moderate support for businesses and moderate marginalisation aid. Mr President, we are not insensitive to the moderate term, but even the modest amount is fine, if applied to a healthy organism. When the disease is serious, more decisive medicines are needed. For this reason we would have preferred a financial one with a less dispersion of the interventions; a maneuver concentrated more on the strengthening of the fundamental themes; a maneuver less tied to that tradition that evokes in many the image of the diligence that is assaulted by Indians or cowboys, and that instead reminds me of that torpedo on the Andes on which the campesinos try to climb, carrying behind everything they can, from domestic animals to agricultural products, to the food. This is the picturesque picture that I attach to the more than 3,000 amendments which, together with our regulations and our parliamentary practices on the work of the Commission and the House, mark the urgent need for reform at the roots of our system. Italy needs stability, the economy needs it, businesses, citizens and the institutions themselves need it. The new majority can give this stability, but stability is not an absolute good. We are not here to defend the fort at all costs, to criticize at all costs the officers or petty officers who defend this fort; we are here with responsibility, with seriousness to help do the right things, more necessary and more appropriate for our country. We are convinced that we support the new understanding not in our interest but in the interest of the country, and the interest of the country is to have a majority that dialogues with itself, that knows the things that must be done, that puts the general interest before the particular interests. Then the new majority must be found, it must find the new reasons for this understanding, it must find in a stronger cohesion the reasons for its unity, the reasons that must be the basis for the growth of our country. Mr President, this new majority, this new understanding, must gather the heads of state's exhortation to have courage. I am thinking again of the central issue, the taxation on the house that, with the new taxes that have already changed several times during the discussion (from TRISE to TUC up to IUC), instead of decreasing the total amount of taxation, obviously with the exclusion of the main house, as from the government's programmatic commitments, and to simplify the whole system, ended up complicated and increased the tax burden at the intersection between IMU second house and new taxation. With the single municipal tax the margins of the municipalities are further expanded and in 2014 the revenue deriving from the taxation of houses, land, sheds and commercial premises risks to exceed 30 billion euros to go even beyond this amount in the following years, from 2015 onwards. In summary, compared to the ICI of the Berlusconi Government of 2011 (9 billion), from 2014 the total revenue on the real estate will be more than tripled: over 30 billion. A de facto asset. The majority commitments - everyone knows - were on a total revenue of 20-21 billion, i.e. the total made with the IMU of Monti minus the first house. The government has disregarded the majority commitments and cheated the Italians. This law, too fragmented, little more than an index with very little substance, is not able to get the country out of the recession by intervening on the central problem of domestic demand. We expected more; from a government of broad understanding we expected more. The government led by Enrico Letta has had in us convinced and responsible supporters; we have believed that, Mr President. The broad understandings were born on the assumption of a strong attempt at national peace. In the absence of a program signed point by point like the one that was the basis of the German Grosse Koalition of 2005, due to the delay accumulated by Bersani's volious attempts, we agreed on a method. And so we had an instrument to orchestrate an effective work of proposal and noble compromise. This instrument is described to President Letta, who dedicated to this topic a precise definition in his long speech for the confidence of 29 April last. He spoke of permanent co-decision places, in which I will participate personally, between the Government and the political forces that have decided to support him. Today, at the most important test of an Executive, the law of stability, we discover a confused Government, unable to implement the measures, unable to trace the bow back to the country; a Government that writes and rewrites, trying to correct in vain its first law of stability. A government that proposes an unacceptable law. How should we read the government's attitude throughout the discussion on the law of stability? How should we read the attitude of the rapporteurs and the President of the Commission? The contextuality of a fundamental passage of any Government, the law of stability, and a striking and shameful fact for a democratic Republic, the vote on the decadence of Senator Silvio Berlusconi, leads us to think of a connection between the two events. So I wonder if the continuous postponements and subsequent cancellations of any confrontation of the Government with our Group, part of the majority, the timing of the presentation of amendments by the Government, the acceleration in the Commission, to the point of ignoring any proposed amendment, are perhaps not linked to the unbridled will to respect anyway the date of 27 November for the most disconcerting vote that has ever been made in an Italian Parliamentary Assembly! [[Applause from the FI-PdL XVII and LAG Groups.]] Tomorrow in the Chamber will be staged the most grotesque episode of the life of the Italian Republic. But we stay here tonight to do our job of responsibility and service for the country. We do not recognize ourselves in the law of stability proposed by the Government. We do not recognize ourselves in the subsequent changes. We do not recognize ourselves in this incredible maxi amendment that has been proposed to us in these hours. We are announcing tonight that we are voting against the law of stability. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII]] and we do not give our vote of confidence to the Letta Government because we believe that this vote of ours must give the Italian citizens an important signal, a warning signal. This law of stability does not deserve our trust. [[Applause from the FI-PdL Group XVII. Congratulations.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2013-11-26-LEG17-Senato-sed-141.ana.u335	Mr Guerrieri Paleopti (PD). Mr President, representatives of the Government, ladies and gentlemen, the Democratic Party Group will vote with conviction in favour of the law of stability and trust in the Government. The text of the maxi amendment tabled by the Government collects much of the work done in recent weeks by the Senate Budget Committee. I would like to thank the majority and opposition senators who have worked in the Commission over the past few weeks, first and foremost President Azzollini and the two rapporteurs, Santini and D'Alì. The quality work carried out has been very useful, because it has led to substantial improvements that were subsequently incorporated in the Government text. As has been the case many other times in the past, the law of stability, precisely because it is complex and because it deals with heterogeneous topics, has been subjected to many criticisms in recent weeks, complaining of shortcomings and shortcomings of various kinds, which have also been taken up by many in today's debate. Of course, some of these points are well founded and it can be agreed that improvements and changes can still be made from the House. But at the same time it should be pointed out that every judgment should be formulated and evaluated taking into account the peculiar phase that is crossed by our country, and above all having full awareness of the problems and constraints of resources available to our economy. Let us remember that the general economic context is characterized, as we know very well, by a very serious economic and social crisis which has been in existence for some time and which in recent years has seen its conditions even worse, especially on the unemployment front. Only in recent months has the fall of productive activity stopped and an air of cautious optimism began to breathe. For now they are only first signs, still weak, but that we want and yet we must try in every way to cultivate and strengthen. This is why we need targeted, determined interventions, precisely because we want to avoid and we want to get out of the conditions of stagnation in which we have been bogged down for a long time. Well, it is against the backdrop of this delicate transitional phase that the bill of stability that we are debating today must be included. It is a maneuver aimed - we know - on the one hand to keep the accounts in order, but on the other to prepare the conditions to engage the recovery transforming it above all into a real growth. I have heard a number of comments from the oppositions on the yet another tax stalemate that would be contained in this maneuver. But beyond these slogans the numbers tell us anything else. After four years of "blood and tear" maneuvers - I remember 100 billion euros of fiscal restrictions from 2010 to 2012 - the law of stability, this law of stability marks a first significant reversal of the trend in the conduct of fiscal policy. The fact that next year the expansionary measures will go beyond the restrictive ones is to be welcomed, I believe, very favorably. Let us also remember that next year there will always be a slight worsening of the public deficit precisely because this deterioration wants to benefit the real economy. I cannot, of course, dwell on the many measures contained in the formulation of the Stability Act, which have been very well illustrated in the course of other interventions today, from the relaxation of the Internal Stability Pact to the financing of investment in infrastructure, the benefits for the capitalization of enterprises, to the funding of the Social Policy Fund. Well, compared to this initial plant - I would like to remind you - the work carried out here in the Senate has allowed to include some important modifications and additions that we consider to improve many parts of the maneuver and that have now been transposed into the text of the maxiemendameento presented by the Government. I remember that in order to hope to transform recovery into a real phase of growth we must somehow support and revitalize aggregate demand, in particular domestic demand, which is made - as we know - of consumption and investment of companies, but at the same time we must remove those structural rigidities on the front of the productive supply that have lowered the potential growth rate of our economy in recent years to almost zero. I want to highlight here three important measures which have been reinforced in the version of the maneuver that we are now about to approve. The first concerns credit to businesses and families. We know that there is an absolute need to increase it if we want the economy to start again in the coming months. Compared to what was originally planned, the intervention here in the Senate has allowed to increase the refinancing of the Guarantee Fund and above all to widen the public guarantee platform for access to the credit of companies, with the involvement of the Cassa depositi e loani and, above all, of the trusts. With the use of modest resources it will thus be possible to obtain a very substantial multiplier effect: we can speak of tens of billions of euros of additional credit, of new loans in the three years 2014-2016. The second measure is certainly the reduction of the tax wedge, which has been mentioned several times and it is true that it is a fundamental node for the growth of our country. It is said that few resources have been allocated: as we know, this is more than EUR 5 billion. Right, but it should also be remembered that for many years the tax wedge had disappeared among the priorities of the governments' agenda. Compared to the original wording we have somehow concentrated the audience of the beneficiaries of tax deductions and thus increased the net benefits, especially for low and medium-low labour incomes. Of course, now it is important that there is a commitment of the Government to centralize the reduction of the tax burden on work and businesses, starting next year, with a path that must be graduated over time, but on which we must concentrate the resources that will be released, also thanks to the spending review. This will mean a significant redistribution of the tax burden in favour of income from work and businesses. Finally, let me also remind you of the single municipal tax. In this case it should not be forgotten that the Government has built a completely federal plant, referring to the autonomy of the municipalities the choices that will concern the construction, over time, of the service tax. It is a restructuring of local property taxation, in the three components: the asset tax, the service tax and the waste tax in some way. Well, it is absolutely not true that we are facing yet another tax stalemate. [[Comments from the LN-Aut Group]] We actually have the certainty that you will be able to pay online or less of what you paid in 2012, but above all important deductions have been introduced in favour of the first-home families with children. Of course we can and must do more on the subject of social policy, where we know that there are important areas of intervention on which to invest resources, for objectives of equity, which we believe are now as important as those of efficiency, for the revival of the economy. In this area, however, let me remind you that there has been the introduction of a first trial of a support income, which will complement the income of families below the poverty line and which have been allocated, over the three years, EUR 120 million. In light of what has been said, it is difficult to understand why Forza Italia decided, on the basis of this maneuver, its exit from the majority. The history of increasing taxes actually has no foundation, if we look at numbers. At the same time, we know that a move towards reducing expenditure and taxes does not give any certainty - as far as the short and medium term are concerned - that it can be transformed into a stimulus to the economy.
